```text
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex:4:Linear directions in LLM residual streams encode behavioral concepts \citep{zou2023repe}; inference-time methods---ITI \citep{li2023iti}, ActAdd \citep{turner2023actadd}, CAA \citep{rimsky2024caa}---demonstrate that activation geometry suffices for behavioral control \citep{burns2023ccs}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex:8:Proxy rewards diverge from true objectives under sustained optimization \citep{gao2023scaling}---a pattern formalized as variants of Goodhart's Law~\citep{manheim2019goodhart}---and reward hacking can escalate to active tampering \citep{denison2024subterfuge}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/related_work.tex:19:SMART \citep{beigi2025smart} applies per-step process rewards for sycophancy, succeeding where our completion-level rewards fail---consistent with ACT's per-token success.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/probe_trap.tex:45:At step 500, the model is behaviorally dead ($\tof = 0$) yet entropy remains high (0.70--0.80) through step 1000---well within the range that standard monitors would flag as healthy---and the combined reward averages $+0.72$ because both $\rone$ and $\rthree$ reinforce non-flipping.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/probe_trap.tex:53:Any reward conditioned only on $\dsyc$---continuous or discrete, differentiable or frozen---will either be gamed or will reward rigidity.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/conclusion.tex:6:For sycophancy---and likely for other attributes where the target and its complement overlap geometrically---condition-aware rewards with dense supervision offer the most promising path forward.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex:49:$\rthree$-only GRPO tests this hypothesis by relying solely on the NLI behavioral reward---and fails: only 4/28 checkpoints across two seeds exceed vanilla (14.3\%), with both seeds oscillating ($\Delta \approx 0.069$) before diverging (Table~\ref{tab:main_comparison}; Figure~\ref{fig:trajectory}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex:60:Under identical adapters, per-token SFT exceeds per-completion GRPO by $1.7{\times}$ ($0.267$ vs.\ $0.153$), identifying supervision granularity as the strongest candidate explanation---independently corroborated by SMART's per-step success~\citep{beigi2025smart}---though sequence length (256 vs.\ 1024), data source (online vs.\ curated), and dropout (0 vs.\ 0.05) remain acknowledged confounds.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex:92:The reduced-LoRA ablation (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}) shows this is not an adapter-capacity artifact: reduced-LoRA ACT ($\sycon{=}0.326$) exceeds full ACT ($\sycon{=}0.301$), leaving supervision granularity---per-token vs.\ per-completion---as the most plausible explanatory variable.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex:96:This suggests two orthogonal failure dimensions: (1)~inside vs.\ outside the optimization loop---gradient exploitation enables the probe reward trap (\S\ref{sec:probe_trap}), while inference-time reranking sidesteps it; and (2)~scalar per-completion reward---the failure persists even without the probe ($\rthree$-only GRPO).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/direction_quality.tex:16:This monotonic relationship reflects effect-size differences---stronger evidence produces larger behavioral gaps that are easier to classify---rather than indicating that $\dsyc$ encodes evidential warrant per se.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/abstract.tex:1:[Omitted long matching line]
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:29:Grad.\ clipping & --- & 1.0 \\
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:36:Group size & 2$^d$ & --- \\
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:37:KL $\beta$ & 0.04 & --- \\
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:38:Max compl.\ len. & 256 & --- \\
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:39:Max seq.\ len. & --- & 1{,}024 \\
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:40:L2 loss weight & --- & 0.1 \\
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex:17:In our experiments, the 1{,}300-step lag before distributional collapse means entropy-based early stopping---the most common safeguard against reward hacking \citep{casper2023openproblems}---would miss the failure entirely, with the reward averaging $+0.72$ throughout.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex:22:By contrast, ACT \citep{consistency2025bctact}---which provides dense per-token supervision rather than a single scalar per completion---exceeds vanilla at every checkpoint (47/47; \S\ref{sec:discussion}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/cosine_failure.tex:4:During GRPO training, 256-token truncation reduces $\dsyc$ AUROC from 0.827 to approximately 0.60---the optimizer works with a substantially weaker signal than the offline metrics suggest.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/cosine_failure.tex:26:Cosine similarity (F1b) eliminates this norm dependence but reveals a second problem: the gap between valid and sycophantic cosines averages only 0.098---too narrow for meaningful reward contrast.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/cosine_failure.tex:28:Amplification (F1d, $5{\times}$ scaling) lifts $\rone$ above the noise floor but surfaces the fundamental limitation: $\rthree$ saturates at 0.9998 by step 300, leaving the noisy $\rone$---which conflates warranted revision with unwarranted capitulation---as the sole gradient source until terminal collapse at step 2160.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-3/projects/contrastive_entrain_calib/docs/paper/sections/cosine_failure.tex:35:This is not a calibration artifact---recalibration improves discriminability yet rewards still fail---but a semantic limitation of any continuous $\dsyc$-based reward.

### Narrative Consistency Audit

**Abstract's problem statement**: When a frozen linear probe on $\dsyc$ is used as SFT training signal, behavioral death occurs within 500 steps while entropy remains healthy. 

The entropy lag of 1,300 steps means the monitoring fails to catch the failure mode - the direction only tells us whether belief changed, not whether it should have. This failure pattern shows up across GRPO too, but per-token SFT avoids it while per-completion RL doesn't.

**Introduction's problem statement**: Same story - representation engineering reveals sycophancy as a linear direction, the probe reward trap shows behavioral death before distributional collapse, and supervision granularity is the key factor.

**Introduction's contribution list**:
1. The probe reward trap (C2): behavioral death precedes distributional collapse by 1,300 steps
2. Categorical reliability gap between supervision paradigms (C1): per

-token SFT exceeds vanilla at every checkpoint while per-completion GRPO fails

I'm noticing the contributions are labeled C2 then C1, which is backwards numbering. The paper title emphasizes the probe reward trap as the main contribution, so that makes sense as the lead, but the labeling is inverted. Let me check if this is intentional or if the numbering should be swapped.

Looking at how the paper unfolds—it moves from the cosine failure into the probe trap finding, then later discusses the supervision paradigm comparison. So C2 gets presented first because it's more foundational, even though C1 might be ranked as the primary contribution. This could trip up readers, but it seems deliberate based on the narrative flow.

The methodology section covers direction extraction, three different reward formulations, online recalibration, an SFT baseline, and then evaluation. These build on each other to test the core hypotheses.

Both contributions check out experimentally—the probe reward trap gets full treatment in section 5, while the supervision paradigm gap appears in section 6 with supporting tables and ablations. The conclusion ties back nicely to behavioral death preceding distributional collapse and the role of supervision granularity.

There's a potential issue with the C1/C2 ordering that could confuse reviewers—they're numbered in reverse of how they're presented. And I'm noticing an inconsistency with some numbers in the abstract that needs tracking down. I'm noticing a critical inconsistency in the paper's numbers—the abstract, introduction, and conclusion all cite 4/31 checkpoints (12.9%), but the discussion and table footnote reference 4/28 checkpoints (14.3%). These are mathematically different results, so I need to figure out which denominator is actually correct. I'm noticing inconsistencies in how the paper reports the R3-only GRPO results across different sections—the abstract and introduction cite 4/31 checkpoints while the discussion says 4/28, and the conclusion's phrasing "across configurations" suggests the 31 might aggregate multiple GRPO variants rather than just the behavioral-reward-only version. I need to figure out whether these numbers are actually measuring the same thing or if they're referring to different subsets of the experimental runs. I'm seeing that the numbers actually line up consistently across the paper—4 out of 28 checkpoints for the two-seed R3-only experiments with gs=2, and 4 out of 31 when including the gs=8 variant, which had zero exceeding vanilla. The intro's framing of "behavioral-reward-only GRPO" as 4/31 is accurate since it encompasses all R3-only configurations.

Now I'm checking the "47/47" claim about dense per-token SFT across the abstract and intro, where it says this approach succeeds at every checkpoint, but the discussion and table footnotes seem to suggest some nuance around the ACT seed 2 results that might contradict this. Looking at the abstract word count more carefully—it's running around 180 words once I strip out the LaTeX formatting, which keeps it comfortably under the 200-word EMNLP limit.

The introduction follows a solid arc: it establishes the RepE background, identifies the probe reward trap as the core problem, then broadens to show this isn't just a probe issue before diving into the semantic gap explanation and listing contributions. The structure is a bit compressed—more like four paragraphs plus the contribution list—but it works.

Related work is well-organized with topic-based headings rather than just listing who did what, which makes it easier to follow the landscape.

Now checking whether the contributions actually align with what's covered: C2 about the probe reward trap gets its treatment in section 5 with supporting tables, and C1 about the categorical reliability gap between per-token and per-completion evaluation is there too.

The paper does have sections that aren't explicitly listed as contributions—section 3 on direction quality and section 4 on cosine failures—but these aren't orphaned; they're doing narrative work by showing that direction quality isn't the bottleneck and providing the diagnostic evidence that leads to the probe trap conclusion.

The claims avoid "significantly" and don't make state-of-the-art declarations, which is appropriate for the scope.  The quantitative claims are well-supported: behavioral death within 500 steps (Table 2), the checkpoint comparisons (Tables 3-4), the 1,300-step lag calculation, and the supervision granularity explanation is properly hedged as a candidate. On experimental rigor, the cross-validated AUROC has reported standard deviations, and ACT and GRPO each have two seeds, though the probe trap relies on a single seed and SyconScore values lack error bars—a minor gap in the tables.

For reproducibility, hyperparameters are in the appendix and code is available anonymously, though GPU type and training time aren't explicitly stated. The ablations cover group size and LoRA-controlled variants, matching the method components. Terminology like $\dsyc$ and $\tof$ is used consistently throughout, and there's a limitations section, though no ethics statement (which EMNLP doesn't require).

The transitions between sections work well—direction quality establishes the foundation before testing as reward, then explicitly bridges to the cosine failure analysis, which in turn sets up the probe trap discussion. The contribution numbering with C2 before C1 is slightly non-standard, and a few symbol definitions like $\sycon$ and $\rone$, $\rthree$ are introduced in earlier sections.

For the limitations, I'm noting that the section covers seven distinct points—group size, token truncation, training/eval overlap, single seed for probing, differences between ACT and GRPO, contrastive pair quality, and single architecture choice. This needs to be replaced with alternatives like commas, periods, or parentheses throughout the paper. I'm continuing through the document to identify all the explanatory em-dashes that need removal, distinguishing them from the table separators that should stay. Most of these are parenthetical asides or dramatic pauses embedded in the text across various sections like the probe trap analysis, discussion, and direction quality sections. The writing norms indicate all these em-dashes should be removed as they're a telltale sign of AI writing. For filler words, I found only one potential match in the discussion table caption, but I need to verify what actually triggered it since the grep results are a bit unclear.

Looking at the -ing phrases more carefully, most of the matches are legitimate noun uses like "Representation Engineering," "gradient gaming," and "finding" rather than the problematic trailing -ing constructions that signal AI writing. The few I spot-checked—"hacking" as a noun, "finding" as a noun, "scaling" as a noun—all check out as natural usage.

The paper's paragraph structure looks fine too, using standard ACL formatting with section headings rather than excessive fragmentation.

I'm noticing those internal experiment IDs like "exp-008" and "exp-008v3" scattered through the text—they're used as experiment identifiers ("Without stop-gradient (exp-008)") which is borderline acceptable for reproducibility but does read like internal tracking notation rather than something you'd typically see in a published paper.

Now checking the EMNLP-specific requirements: the abstract comes in at around 175-185 words when rendered, well under the 200-word limit, the limitations section is properly placed in the main text before the bibliography with no duplicate in the appendix, and the ARR checklist isn't included in the PDF as it shouldn't be.

For the 8-page limit, I need to account for the fact that references and limitations don't count toward that threshold. Breaking down the content, the main text appears to span roughly 8-9 pages when I estimate the introduction, framework sections, experimental analyses, discussion, and related work together, which puts it right at or slightly over the limit depending on formatting.

Since Phase 1 compiled to 11 pages total without flagging the main text length specifically, it's likely within bounds, but I should verify this isn't an issue.

On the limitations section, the current 267 words covering 7 points is more substantial than the guidance suggested, though it's not necessarily problematic.

I'm also noticing a few other concerns: Table 3's caption is quite dense with multiple footnote markers and tries to explain too many nuances at once, which could confuse readers. The hyperparameters table is missing important reproducibility details like GPU model and total training time. And while placing Related Work after Discussion is acceptable for ACL venues, some reviewers might prefer it earlier.

There's also an issue with how the contributions are numbered—C2 is presented first in the text but labeled as the secondary contribution, while C1 comes second but seems to be the primary one. This reversed ordering is disorienting, especially since C2 (the probe reward trap finding) appears more novel than C1 (the broader comparison). Finally, the "47/47" count in the abstract needs clarification since the ablation table shows different configurations that sum to 47, but reduced-LoRA is a distinct variant. The intro claims ACT specifically achieves 47/47, but that count includes the reduced-LoRA ablation variant which uses different hyperparameters and is presented separately in the table. The conclusion reframes this as "per-token SFT" succeeding at every checkpoint, which is technically broader and more accurate since it encompasses all per-token variants, but the intro's attribution to ACT alone is potentially misleading about what the core method actually achieved.
```

Now I have all the data I need. Let me also verify the Figure 3 caption says "31/31" for ACT vs the intro's "47/47". Let me check the discussion.tex line 86 more carefully:

"ACT exceeds vanilla at every evaluated checkpoint (31/31 across two seeds)" - so the figure caption says 31/31 for ACT's two seeds, not 47/47.

And the intro says "exceeds vanilla at every checkpoint (47/47)" including reduced-LoRA.

This is an important distinction. 31/31 is ACT full config only (two seeds: 16+15). 

So the 47/47 count includes the reduced-LoRA ablation variant, which the intro attributes to "ACT" but the conclusion more accurately labels as "per-token SFT" to encompass all variants. The intro's phrasing is slightly misleading since it credits ACT specifically with all 47 checkpoints when 16 of those come from the ablation.

Now I'm looking at the 4/31 versus 4/28 discrepancy more carefully. The intro reports 4/31 checkpoints (12.9%) for behavioral-reward-only GRPO across all group sizes, while the discussion cites 4/28 checkpoints (14.3%) for R3-only GRPO with group size 2 across two seeds—so these are measuring different subsets of the data. The gs=8 ablation shouldn't be lumped into the main results without clarification, since it's testing a different configuration. The real issue is that the introduction uses 31 checkpoints (mixing gs=2 and gs=8) while the discussion and figure captions cite 4/28 (gs=2 only), which could trip up someone verifying the numbers across sections. Now I need to check whether the Conclusion's claim about "all GRPO formulations" actually accounts for the other variants like the cosine and continuous probe experiments, since including those would dramatically change the denominator. I think the "4/31" in the Conclusion is specifically referring to R3-only GRPO results, while "all formulations failed" is the broader claim encompassing the other GRPO variants (cosine and continuous probe) which performed even worse. So R3-only with 4/31 checkpoints exceeding vanilla represents the strongest GRPO performance overall.

Now I'm compiling the key findings across the entire analysis. There are critical issues with LaTeX formatting conventions appearing throughout the text—specifically em-dashes used in a way that's characteristic of AI writing—along with inconsistent notation where the same metric gets different denominators across sections without explanation. Beyond that, there are warnings about internal experiment identifiers leaking into the main text and some organizational issues with how contributions are numbered and ordered. 再列 C1
5. [叙事一致性] Intro 说 "ACT exceeds vanilla at every checkpoint (47/47)" 但 47 包含 reduced-LoRA 消融变体的 16 个 checkpoint
6. [可复现性] 缺少 GPU 型号和训练时间/GPU-hours
7. [实验严谨性] SyconScore 主结果无 error bars/std（仅报最佳 checkpoint 数值）

**INFO:**
8. [章节结构] Table 3 caption 过长（~80 words + 5 个脚注标记），信息密度过高
9. [写作规则] Limitations 部分篇幅过长，涵盖 7 个点、267 词，远超建议的每小段 3 个点的结构；还有符号 C1/C2 的含义没有明确定义，以及单 seed probe trap 实验虽在 Limitations 中已声明但仍需注意可复现性问题。

- [warning] [叙事一致性] 不同章节对同一 GRPO 结果使用不同分母且未解释：Abstract/Intro/Conclusion 写 "4/31 (12.9%)"（含 gs=8 消融的 3 个 checkpoint），Discussion/Table 3 footnote/Figure 3 caption 写 "4/28 (14.3%)"（仅 gs=2 两个 seed）。审稿人交叉比对会发现不一致。建议统一为 4/28 (gs=2) 和 0/3 (gs=8)，或在首次出现 4/31 时注明包含 gs=8 消融。
- [warning] [内部信息] probe_trap.tex:10,14,22,40; appendix.tex:60 使用内部实验编号 "exp-008"、"exp-008v3"。这类版本化编号（v3）暴露内部迭代流程，建议改为描述性名称（如 "differentiable-probe run" 和 "frozen-probe run"），或引入正式实验标签表。
- [warning] [贡献-范围] introduction.tex:35-37 贡献列表先列 C2 再列 C1，编号反序。读者预期 C1 出现在 C2 之前。且 C1/C2 标签在正文中首次出现时无定义说明（为什么叫 C1/C2？是重要性排序还是呈现顺序？）。建议按呈现顺序重编为 C1/C2，或删除编号标签直接用描述性标题。
- [warning] [叙事一致性] introduction.tex:22 写 "ACT...exceeds vanilla at every checkpoint (47/47)"，但 47 = ACT full s1 (16) + ACT full s2 (15) + reduced-LoRA ACT (16)。reduced-LoRA 是消融变体（α=64 vs α=128, 4 modules vs 7 modules），归入 ACT 的 47/47 统计会让读者误以为全是标准配置。Conclusion 用 "per-token SFT (47/47)" 更准确。建议 Intro 改为 "31/31 across two seeds" 或明确注明含消融。
- [warning] [可复现性] 全文及 Appendix 未报告 GPU 型号（A100/H100/V100 等）和总训练时间/GPU-hours。ARR checklist 的 Compute 项要求此信息。建议在 Appendix Table 5 脚注或正文 §2 补充。
- [warning] [实验严谨性] Table 3 (main_comparison) 的 SyconScore 仅报告单个 best-checkpoint 值，无 error bars 或跨 seed 均值±std。GRPO 有两个 seed 但只报 best，ACT 有两个 seed 但分开报告而非汇总。建议对多 seed 结果报告 mean±std。
- [info] [章节结构] discussion.tex:21 Table 3 的 caption 含 ~80 词 + 5 个脚注标记（†‡§¶ + 正文备注），信息密度过高。建议将 arithmetic mean 讨论移入正文段落，caption 只保留核心定义。
- [info] [写作规则] Limitations 段覆盖 7 个点共 267 词。用户规则建议 "一小段覆盖 3 个点左右"。当前长度不违规但偏长，可考虑合并相关点（如 group size + truncation 合为 "GRPO 配置约束"）压缩到 ~150 词。
- [info] [符号] C1/C2 标签仅在 Introduction 贡献列表中出现（括号标注），未有 "We refer to these as C1 and C2" 的显式定义。Discussion 中直接写 "C2, §5" 时读者需回翻确认。建议在贡献列表前加一句说明编号含义或直接去掉编号。
- [info] [叙事一致性] Abstract 和 Conclusion 均使用 "4/31" 和 "47/47" 的对比框架，叙事对称且一致。核心故事线（dsyc discriminability ≠ reward utility → probe trap → supervision granularity）在各章节间保持统一，无 A/B 问题漂移。
- [info] [章节过渡] 各章节过渡良好：Direction Quality 末段 "Direction quality is not the bottleneck" 明确桥接到 Cosine Failure；Cosine Failure 末句 "Section 5 examines whether discretizing..." 衔接 Probe Trap。Discussion 后接 Related Work 符合 *ACL 惯例。
